\widehat{{F}}
(as a marked boundary component) and
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{n}}}}^{1}
, this action reduces to moving subspaces of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{n}}}}^{1}
around by means of the representation
\widehat{{L}}\curvearrowright\widehat{{\mathfrak{{n}}}}^{1}
. By the equivariance of
\mathcal{{H}}
, for every
g\in\widehat{{L}}
, the action of
\mathcal{{H}}(\widehat{{F}},\Ad(g)(\mathfrak{{w}}))
is orbit-equivalent to that of
\mathcal{{H}}(\widehat{{F}},\mathfrak{{w}})
. This allows us to strengthen part (
) of Theorem
3.3
, where only the congruence with respect to
K_{j}=\widehat{{K}}
was considered. We will make use of this in Corollary
4.15
below.

Observation 4.13
.

Let us consider all possible C1-actions that can be obtained by performing the nilpotent construction over a given
\widehat{{F}}\in\mathcal{{B}}
. Fix some
(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j})\in\mathcal{{F}}^{-1}(\widehat{{F}})
. We know three things:
\widehat{{L}}=\widehat{{M}}\times\widehat{{A}}
,
\widehat{{A}}
acts transitively on the set of noncompact parts
\widehat{{B}}\subset\widehat{{F}}
(this action looks like shifting the noncompact parts of
\widehat{{F}}
by transvections along an orthonormal geodesic), and
\widehat{{G}}^{\prime}\subseteq\widehat{{M}}
acts transitively on the set of its own Iwasawa decompositions. In view of Proposition
4.8
, we see that every element
(\overline{o},\overline{\mathfrak{{a}}},\overline{\Upsigma}^{+},\overline{\upalpha}_{l},\overline{\mathfrak{{w}}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
with
(\overline{o},\overline{\mathfrak{{a}}},\overline{\Upsigma}^{+},\overline{\upalpha}_{l})\in\mathcal{{F}}^{-1}(\widehat{{F}})
can be moved by
\widehat{{L}}
to a tuple of the form
(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})
. Essentially, this means that by fixing
o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},
and
\upalpha_{j}
and going through all possible admissible and protohomogeneous subspaces
\mathfrak{{w}}\subset\mathfrak{{n}}_{j}^{1}
of
\codim\geq 2
, we exhaust all C1-actions that can be obtained from
\widehat{{F}}
via nilpotent construction.

Having all these preparations finally ready, we can prove the main result of this subsection:

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
M=G/K
be a symmetric space of noncompact type, and let
\upchi=(\widehat{{F}},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\mathcal{{N}}
be some nilpotent construction data. Then there exist
\widetilde{{\upchi}}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
with
\mathcal{{G}}(\widetilde{{\upchi}})=\upchi
and a maximal abelian subspace
\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}
such that the normalizer
N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
contains a solvable subalgebra
V\oplus\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}
, where
V\subseteq\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
projects surjectively onto
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
.

In a nutshell, this proposition says that in the nilpotent construction, we can always assume that
N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
contains the whole nilpotent part
\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}
of the Iwasawa decomposition of
\mathfrak{{g}}^{\prime}_{j}
and “almost contains” its abelian part
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
. For the sake of brevity, if
(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
satisfies this property—that is, if
N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
has a subalgebra of the form
V\oplus\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}
as above—we will call it
positive
.

Proof of Proposition
4.14
.

Let
(\widehat{{F}},\mathfrak{{w}})
be as in the proposition. By admissibility, the subgroup
N_{\widehat{{L}}}(\mathfrak{{w}})\subseteq\widehat{{L}}
acts transitively on the extended boundary component
\widehat{{F}}
. Pick a noncompact part
\widehat{{B}}
of
\widehat{{F}}
. Since we are dealing with maximal boundary components, we can use reformulation (iv) of the admissibility condition (see p.
(iv)
), which in our redundancy-free setting reads: the subgroup
N_{\widehat{{M}}}(\mathfrak{{w}})\subseteq\widehat{{M}}
acts transitively on the boundary component
\widehat{{B}}
. Recall that
\widehat{{B}}
can be represented by the Riemannian symmetric pair
(\widehat{{M}}^{0},\widehat{{K}}^{0})
. The ineffectiveness kernel
\mathfrak{{i}}
of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{m}}}}
is the sum of its center
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{z}}}}
and the compact semisimple part of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}
. Consequently,
\mathfrak{{i}}
is compact, so we can apply Proposition
4.6
to the normalizer
N^{0}_{\widehat{{M}}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
. For starters, it provides us with an Iwasawa decomposition of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}^{\prime}
. Together with
\widehat{{B}}
, it specifies a tuple
\widetilde{{\upchi}}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
with
\mathcal{{G}}(\widetilde{{\upchi}})=\upchi
—as follows from Proposition
4.8
. By applying Proposition
4.6
further, we also get a maximal abelian subspace
\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}^{j}
in the
\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}
-part
\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}^{j}
of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{{g}}^{\prime}_{j}
and a maximal abelian subspace
\mathfrak{{t}}
in
\mathfrak{{i}}=Z_{\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}}(\mathfrak{{g}}^{\prime}_{j})
. In the end,
N_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{{m}}}}}(\mathfrak{{w}})=N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
contains a solvable subalgebra of the form
V\oplus\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}
, where

V\subseteq\mathfrak{{t}}\oplus\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}^{j}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}\subseteq Z_{\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}}(\mathfrak{{g}}^{\prime}_{j})\oplus\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}^{j}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}=\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}

projects surjectively onto
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
along
\mathfrak{{t}}\oplus\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}^{j}
. It simply remains to notice that
\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}=\mathfrak{{t}}\oplus\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}^{j}
is a maximal abelian subspace in
\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

We can also look at Proposition
4.14
from a slightly different angle. Suppose we start with a tuple
\widetilde{{\upchi}}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
, and write
\upchi=\mathcal{{G}}(\widetilde{{\upchi}})=(F_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})
. Proposition
4.14
asserts that there exists a
positive
tuple
\overline{\widetilde{{\upchi}}}=(\overline{o},\overline{\mathfrak{{a}}},\overline{\Upsigma}^{+},\overline{\upalpha}_{l},\overline{\mathfrak{{w}}})
in the fiber of
\mathcal{{G}}
over
\upchi
. But thanks to Observation
4.13
, we can move
\overline{\widetilde{{\upchi}}}
to a tuple of the form
\widetilde{{\upchi}}^{\prime}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime})
by an element of
\widehat{{L}}=L_{j}
(or in our case even of
\widehat{{G}}^{\prime}=G^{\prime}_{j}
). Now, whether or not an element of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
is positive is clearly preserved by the action of
G
on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
. Since both
\mathcal{{G}}
and
\mathcal{{H}}
are
G
-equivariant, we arrive at the following alternative formulation of Proposition
4.14
:

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
M=G/K
be a symmetric space of noncompact type and
\widetilde{{\upchi}}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
some nilpotent construction data. Then there exists a subspace
\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{{n}}_{j}^{1}
congruent to
\mathfrak{{w}}
via
G^{\prime}_{j}

(
and thus also admissible and protohomogeneous
)
such that
N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime})
contains a solvable subalgebra of the form
V\oplus\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}
, where
V\subseteq\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
projects surjectively onto
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
, and
\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}
is a maximal abelian subspace of
\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}
. The C1-actions arising from
\mathfrak{{w}}
and
\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}
are orbit-equivalent.

4.3.
Root vector elimination

In this final part of the section, we will see how the condition of positivity on nilpotent construction data
(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})
puts strong restrictions on the subspace
\mathfrak{{w}}
and forces it to be positioned well with respect to the restricted root space decomposition. Given a subspace
\mathfrak{{w}}\subseteq\mathfrak{{n}}_{j}^{1}
and
\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}
, we denote
\bm{\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}}=\mathfrak{{w}}\cap\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
M=G/K
be a symmetric space of noncompact type and consider some nilpotent construction data
(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
. The following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

\mathfrak{{w}}=\bigoplus_{\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}}\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}
.

(2)

\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}\subseteq N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
.

Proof.

One direction is obvious: for every
H\in\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
and
\uplambda\in\Upsigma
,
\ad(H)
acts on
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
as the multiplication by
\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\uplambda}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt
, hence
\ad(H)
preserves each
\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}
. If
\mathfrak{{w}}
decomposes as the sum of
\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}
’s, then
\ad(H)
preserves it and so
H
lies in the normalizer. Conversely, assume
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}\subseteq N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
. Since
H^{j}
always acts on
\mathfrak{{n}}_{j}^{1}
as the identity, we have that the whole
\mathfrak{{a}}
normalizes
\mathfrak{{w}}
. Take any
X\in\mathfrak{{w}}
and write it as
X=\sum_{\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}}X_{\uplambda}
, where
X_{\uplambda}\in\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
. It suffices to show that
X_{\uplambda}\in\mathfrak{{w}}
for an arbitrary
\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}
. Take any other
\upgamma\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}
. Since
\uplambda
and
\upgamma
cannot be proportional, there exists a vector
H\in\mathfrak{{a}}
such that
\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\upgamma}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt=0
but
\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\uplambda}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt=1
. (Note that we might have
2\uplambda\in\Upsigma
if the root system is not reduced, but that root would then lie in
\Updelta_{j}^{2}
, not
\Updelta_{j}^{1}
, so we could not take it as
\upgamma
.) We can replace
X
with
[H,X]\in\mathfrak{{w}}
: its
\upgamma
-component is now zero but its
\uplambda
-component is still
X_{\uplambda}
. By repeating this trick with the other roots in
\Updelta_{j}^{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to2.95pt{\vbox to5.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.72499pt\lower-0.22499pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.45pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.77 0 L -0.69 6.92}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to1.9pt{\vbox to3.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.08 0 L 0 4.15}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\hskip-0.8pt\left\{\hskip 0.29999pt{\uplambda}\hskip 0.29999pt\right\}
, we obtain
X_{\uplambda}\in\mathfrak{{w}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Note that we did not actually use the fact that
\mathfrak{{w}}
is admissible or protohomogeneous. We will now prove the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
M=G/K
be a symmetric space of noncompact type and
\widetilde{{\upchi}}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
some nilpotent construction data. If
\widetilde{{\upchi}}
is positive, then it satisfies the conditions of Lemma
4.16
.

Proof.

We will show that
\widetilde{{\upchi}}
satisfies condition (
) of Lemma
4.16
. According to the positivity assumption on
\widetilde{{\upchi}}
, there exist a maximal abelian subspace
\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}
and a subspace
V\subseteq\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
projecting surjectively onto
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}
such that
V\oplus\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}\subseteq N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
. Recall that the adjoint representation of
\mathfrak{{k}}_{0}
on
\mathfrak{{g}}
preserves each root space and is orthogonal with respect to the inner product
B_{\uptheta}
. Since
\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}
is abelian, each root space
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
can be decomposed orthogonally as
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}=\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{0}\oplus\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{k_{\uplambda}}
so that the following holds:

(1)

For each
1\leq i\leq k_{\uplambda}
,
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{i}
is 2-dimensional
8
8

8

One should be able to show that
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{0}
has dimension 1 if the multiplicity of
\uplambda
is odd and 0 otherwise, but we do not need that here.
.

(2)

For every
T\in\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}
,
\ad(T)
preserves each summand
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{i}
and is zero on
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{0}
.

(3)

Fix an orthogonal complex structure
J_{\uplambda}
on
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{k_{\uplambda}}
such that each
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{i}
becomes a complex subspace, and write
J_{\uplambda}^{i}={\left.\kern-1.2ptJ_{\uplambda}\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{i}}}
. Then for every
T\in\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}
,
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\ad(T)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}}}=c_{1}J_{\uplambda}^{1}+\cdots+c_{k_{\uplambda}}J_{\uplambda}^{k_{\uplambda}}
for some
c_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Given a vector
X\in\mathfrak{{g}}
, we write
X_{\uplambda}
and
X_{\uplambda}^{i}
for its orthogonal projections in
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
and
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{i}
, respectively. Observe that for every
A\in\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{{a}}
,
\ad(A)
preserves not only each root space
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
but in fact each subspace
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}^{i}
. In particular, if
X\in\mathfrak{{g}}
has
X_{\uplambda}^{i}=0
, then so does
[A,X]
.

Given
\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}
, we write
W_{\uplambda}
for the orthogonal projection of
\mathfrak{{w}}
in
\mathfrak{{g}}_{\uplambda}
. We have
\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}\subseteq W_{\uplambda}
, and our goal is to show that
\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}=W_{\uplambda}
for an arbitrary
\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}
(which we fix for the rest of the proof). Assume the converse and pick a vector
X\in\mathfrak{{w}}
with
X_{\uplambda}\in W_{\uplambda}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

To begin with, we introduce an auxiliary subspace
U_{\uplambda}=W_{\uplambda}\ominus(\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}\oplus\mathbb{R}X_{\uplambda})
; we thus have an orthogonal decomposition

. There exists a vector

\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\upgamma}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt=0
but
\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\uplambda}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt=1
. We have the subspace
V\oplus\mathbb{R}H^{j}\subseteq N_{\mathfrak{{l}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
that projects surjectively onto
\mathfrak{{a}}
. Consequently, there exists
T\in\mathfrak{{t}}_{0}
such that
A=T+H\in N_{\mathfrak{{l}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}})
. We are going to use the operator
\ad(A)
to eliminate undesired components of
X
. By construction,
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\ad(A)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathfrak{{g}}_{\upgamma}}}={\left.\kern-1.2pt\ad(T)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathfrak{{g}}_{\upgamma}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\upgamma}}c_{i}J_{\upgamma}^{i}
for some
c_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
. Consider the vector
[A,X]\in\mathfrak{{w}}
. We have
[A,X]_{\upgamma}^{0}=0
. On the other hand, since
\ad(T)
is a skew-symmetric operator, the orthogonal projection of
[A,X]
in
\mathbb{R}X_{\uplambda}
is given by
\pr_{\mathbb{R}X_{\uplambda}}([H,X_{\uplambda}])=\pr_{\mathbb{R}X_{\uplambda}}(X_{\uplambda})=X_{\uplambda}
. This means that
[A,X]_{\uplambda}\in W_{\uplambda}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

. Therefore, we may simply assume from the outset that

. Suppose that

that satisfies conditions (
) and (
) on

, but instead of (
) we just require

.

We again consider the vector
[A,X]\in\mathfrak{{w}}
. We have:

\begin{cases}[A,X]_{\uplambda}=[T,X_{\uplambda}]+X_{\uplambda},\\
[A,X]_{\upgamma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\upgamma}}c_{i}J_{\upgamma}^{i}X_{\upgamma}^{i}.\end{cases}

As we already noted earlier,
[T,X_{\uplambda}]\perp X_{\uplambda}
and hence
[A,X]_{\uplambda}\in W_{\uplambda}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

. If

, we simply let

, which does the trick. Otherwise, assuming

, we introduce

and thus

. We split the argument into two cases.

Case 1:

[T,X_{\uplambda}]\in\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}\Leftrightarrow\pr_{U_{\uplambda}}(X^{\prime}_{\uplambda})=0\Leftrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\uplambda}=X_{\uplambda}
. Consider

:

\begin{cases}[A,X^{\prime}]_{\uplambda}=[T,X_{\uplambda}]+X_{\uplambda},\\

We let

. By construction,

but the orthogonal projection of
Z
in
\mathbb{R}X_{\uplambda}
equals
(1+c_{l}^{2})X_{\uplambda}\neq 0
. This means
Z_{\uplambda}\in W_{\uplambda}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }

and so

satisfies the required conditions.

Case 2:

[T,X_{\uplambda}]\not\in\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}\Leftrightarrow\pr_{U_{\uplambda}}(X^{\prime}_{\uplambda})\neq 0\Leftrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\uplambda}\neq X_{\uplambda}
. Let us then denote
V=\pr_{U_{\uplambda}}(X^{\prime}_{\uplambda})
. In this case, we also start by considering the vector
[A,X^{\prime}]\in\mathfrak{{w}}
:

\begin{cases}[A,X^{\prime}]_{\uplambda}=[T,V]+[T,X_{\uplambda}]+V+X_{\uplambda},\\
[A,X^{\prime}]_{\upgamma}=-\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\upgamma}}c_{i}^{2}X_{\upgamma}^{i}.\end{cases}

Let us adjust
[A,X^{\prime}]
to make it orthogonal to
\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}
. Consider the vector
X^{\prime\prime}=[A,X^{\prime}]-\pr_{\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}}([A,X^{\prime}])\in\mathfrak{{w}}
. We have:

\displaystyle X^{\prime\prime}_{\uplambda}

\displaystyle=[A,X^{\prime}]_{\uplambda}-\pr_{\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}}([A,X^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=([T,V]-\pr_{\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}}([T,V]))+([T,X_{\uplambda}]-\pr_{\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}}([T,X_{\uplambda}]))+V+X_{\uplambda}

\displaystyle=([T,V]-\pr_{\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}}([T,V]))+2V+X_{\uplambda}.

In the last line, the summand in the parentheses is orthogonal to
V
, and so is the last summand
X_{\uplambda}
. Consequently, the orthogonal projection of
X^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathbb{R}V
equals
2V\neq 0
. We then let
Z=X^{\prime\prime}+c_{l}^{2}X
; this vector satisfies
Z_{\upgamma}^{l}=0
but has the same nonzero projection in
\mathbb{R}V
. The latter means that
Z_{\uplambda}\in W_{\uplambda}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to2.95pt{\vbox to5.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.72499pt\lower-0.22499pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.45pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.77 0 L -0.69 6.92}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to1.9pt{\vbox to3.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.08 0 L 0 4.15}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\mathfrak{{w}}_{\uplambda}
, so
Z
meets the desired requirements. This completes the proof.
∎

Combining Corollary
4.15
, Proposition
4.17
and Lemma
4.16
, we obtain the main result of this section:

Theorem 4.18
.

Let
M=G/K
be a symmetric space of noncompact type and
\widetilde{{\upchi}}=(o,\mathfrak{{a}},\Upsigma^{+},\upalpha_{j},\mathfrak{{w}})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{{N}}}}
some nilpotent construction data. Then there exists a subspace
\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{{n}}_{j}^{1}
congruent to
\mathfrak{{w}}
via
G^{\prime}_{j}

(
and thus also admissible and protohomogeneous
)
such that
N_{\mathfrak{{m}}_{j}}(\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime})
contains
\mathfrak{{a}}^{j}\oplus\mathfrak{{n}}^{j}
. The subspace
\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}
splits as
\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{\uplambda\in\Updelta_{j}^{1}}\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}_{\uplambda}
. The C1-actions arising from
\mathfrak{{w}}
and
\mathfrak{{w}}^{\prime}
are orbit-equivalent.

5.
Totally geodesic submanifolds and the singular orbit

In this section, we make a slight detour and prove Theorem
B
—a structural result that relates singular orbits of cohomogeneity-one actions on a symmetric space
M=G/K
of noncompact type, as well as totally geodesic submanifolds therein, to an Iwasawa decomposition of
G
and allows to represent these types of submanifolds neatly via the solvable model of
M
. We begin with totally geodesic submanifolds.

Let
M=G/K
be a symmetric space of noncompact type and
S\subseteq M
a complete connected totally geodesic submanifold. Fix a point
o\in S
and write
S_{nc}
and
S_{0}
for the noncompact and flat parts of
S
passing through
o
, respectively. Having fixed the base point, we have the induced Cartan decomposition
\mathfrak{{g}}=\mathfrak{{k}}\oplus\mathfrak{{p}}
and an identification
\mathfrak{{p}}\cong T_{o}M
, so we can introduce the Lie triple systems
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{nc}=T_{o}S_{nc},\,\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{0}=T_{o}S_{0},
and
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}=T_{o}S=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{nc}\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{0}
in
\mathfrak{{p}}
. It follows from the standard theory of symmetric spaces that
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc}=[\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{nc},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{nc}]\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{nc}
is a semisimple subalgebra of
\mathfrak{{g}}
,
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{0}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{0}
is an abelian subalgebra commuting with
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc}
, and thus
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc}\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{0}=[\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}]\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}
is a reductive subalgebra. The corresponding connected Lie subgroups
\widetilde{{G}}_{nc},\widetilde{{G}}_{0},
and
\widetilde{{G}}=\widetilde{{G}}_{nc}\times\widetilde{{G}}_{0}
of
G
have
S_{nc},S_{0},
and
S
as their
o
-orbits, respectively. One can show that these subgroups are closed. (For
\widetilde{{G}}_{nc}
, see
[
Mos50
, Sect. 6]
, the rest then follows from the global Cartan decomposition, see
[
Kna02
, Th. 6.31(c)]
.) Pick a maximal abelian subspace
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}
in
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}
and then enlarge it to a maximal abelian subspace
\mathfrak{{a}}
in
\mathfrak{{p}}
. Note that we necessarily have
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{0}
, where
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}
is maximal abelian in
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{nc}
and
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{0}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{p}}}}_{0}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{0}
. We have an orthogonal decomposition
\mathfrak{{a}}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{0}\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}^{\perp}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}\oplus\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{\perp}
. (As always, we fix
B_{\uptheta}
as a default inner product on
\mathfrak{{g}}
.) If we identify
\mathfrak{{a}}
and
\mathfrak{{a}}^{*}
by means of
B_{\uptheta}
, we have an induced orthogonal decomposition
\mathfrak{{a}}^{*}=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}\oplus(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{\perp})^{*}
. Let us denote the restricted root systems of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc}
and
\mathfrak{{g}}
by
\widetilde{{\Upsigma}}\subseteq\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}
and
\Upsigma\subset\mathfrak{{a}}^{*}
, respectively.

Our first step is to obtain a relation between the restricted root space decompositions of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc}
and
\mathfrak{{g}}
. Pick a root
\widetilde{{\upalpha}}\in\widetilde{{\Upsigma}}
. Take any vectors
H\in\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}
and
X\in(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc})_{\widetilde{{\upalpha}}}
. By decomposing
X=\sum_{\upalpha\in\Upsigma_{0}}X_{\upalpha}
with respect to the restricted root space decomposition of
\mathfrak{{g}}
, we can compute:

\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\widetilde{{\upalpha}}}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004pt\sum_{\upalpha\in\Upsigma_{0}}X_{\upalpha}=[H,X]=\sum_{\upalpha\in\Upsigma_{0}}\langle\hskip 1.0pt{\upalpha}\hskip 0.5pt,{H}\hskip 1.0pt\rangle\hskip 0.20004ptX_{\upalpha}.

Since this holds for every
H\in\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}
, we deduce that
X_{\upalpha}=0
whenever the restriction of
\upalpha
to
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}
differs from
\widetilde{{\upalpha}}
. In particular,
X_{0}=0
. If we introduce
\Upsigma(\widetilde{{\upalpha}})=\big\{\upalpha\in\Upsigma\mid{\left.\kern-1.2pt\upalpha\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}}}=\widetilde{{\upalpha}}\big\}
, then our finding can be reformulated as

(8)

(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{g}}}}_{nc})_{\widetilde{{\upalpha}}}\subseteq\bigoplus_{\upalpha\in\Upsigma(\widetilde{{\upalpha}})}\mathfrak{{g}}_{\upalpha}.

For the sake of convenience, we introduce another piece of notation:

{\left.\kern-1.2pt\Upsigma\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}}}=\big\{{\left.\kern-1.2pt\upalpha\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}}}\mid\upalpha\in\Upsigma\big\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.6pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.84 -2.08 L 0 10.03}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.08 0 L 0 4.15}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\hskip-0.8pt\left\{\hskip 0.29999pt{0}\hskip 0.29999pt\right\}.

This can also be thought of as the orthogonal projection of
\Upsigma
in
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}
(with zero removed if necessary). The inclusion (
8
) then implies
\widetilde{{\Upsigma}}\subseteq{\left.\kern-1.2pt\Upsigma\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}}}
. Next, we are going to show that one can make a choice of positive roots for these two root systems in a compatible manner. We can choose a vector
H^{\prime}\in\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}
such that its annulator in
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}
does not intersect
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\Upsigma\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{*}}}
. Unfortunately,
H^{\prime}
can still vanish on some roots in
\Upsigma
, namely on
\Upsigma\cap(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{\perp})^{*}
. To remedy this, we pick another vector
H^{\prime\prime}\in\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{\perp}
satisfying the following two properties:

(1)

The annulator of
H^{\prime\prime}
in
(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{\perp})^{*}
does not intersect
\Upsigma\cap(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{{a}}}}_{nc}^{\perp})^{*}
.

(2)

For every
\upalpha\in\Upsigma\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to8.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.5pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
